3.  **`e6d7`**: Moving the King to d7 places it behind the Black Bishop on c6. This diagonal offers the Bishop support and remoteness from the White Queen.

Between the two safe squares, `e6d7` seems strategically sounder as it utilises the `c6` Bishop's square and creates space. Additionally, the move `c6d7` was available but the King must move to get out of check.
